TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 19098 ms + 1076 ms + 13172 ms = 33346 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 33346 ms + 9405 ms (59 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 42751 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 43288 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 10207 ms + 581 ms + 3763 ms = 14551 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 14551 ms + 2373 ms (27 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 16924 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 6637 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 20917 ms + 1112 ms + 13199 ms = 35228 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 35228 ms + 9578 ms (60 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 44807 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 43288 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 24005 ms + 1046 ms + 15989 ms = 41040 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 41040 ms + 4289 ms (57 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 45329 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 6637 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 17270 ms + 1081 ms + 16755 ms = 35105 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 35105 ms + 9578 ms (60 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 44683 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 43288 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 18138 ms + 1136 ms + 11467 ms = 30741 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 30741 ms + 4289 ms (57 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 35030 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 6637 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 38740 ms + 126 ms + 27948 ms = 66814 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 66814 ms + 9405 ms (59 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 76219 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 43288 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

column sums		 27582 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL
incl. fall-through cost		 50243 ms vs Baseline 6637 ms

Reading the totals

TP TOTAL = sum of pack/file fetch + extract + harness across served-from-pack assets = 27582 ms + 129 ms + 18243 ms = 45954 ms

TP EFFECTIVE TOTAL = TP TOTAL + sum of baseline-fetch ms for assets TP fell through on
= 45954 ms + 4289 ms (57 fall-throughs paid network cost) = 50243 ms

BL TOTAL = sum of all asset-fetch ms in baseline = 6637 ms

These are column sums — overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Use iteration durations as the real wall-clock answer; use these to understand where time is spent.

Reading this table

  • Pack/file fetch+decode — full/stream variants show the time this request waited for pack initialization. Range-stream variants show metadata wait plus this file's own HTTP Range fetch. miss is the first initializer, pending waited for that initializer, and hit found initialized metadata/pack already reusable.
  • Baseline asset fetch — time to fetch this single asset over the wire in baseline mode. Adjacent for direct comparison.
  • Extract — pack.extract(file), in-memory, sub-millisecond.
  • Harness IPC tax — total − fetch − extract. Pure overhead from this measurement rig: Playwright routing, page.evaluate CDP roundtrip, browser-to-Node string transfer, route.fulfill, and queueing when many requests fire concurrently. Zero in a real Service-Worker deployment.
  • TokenPack total — fetch + extract + harness tax. The wall-clock time the asset was paused in our handler.
  • Green/red coloring compares real work (fetch + extract) vs baseline fetch — i.e., what TokenPack would cost without the harness tax. Green on the TokenPack side = real work was faster than the baseline fetch.
  • TP did not load — baseline fetched this URL, but TokenPack never even requested it. Means the page broke during TP boot before reaching the code that imports this asset (likely a pack-decode failure or wrong response headers earlier in the load).
  • TP-only (not in BL) — TokenPack requested this URL but baseline never did. Different code path got executed (feature flag, A/B variant, error-recovery branch).

Column sums overstate wall-clock because requests run in parallel. Iteration durations are the real answer.

Legend
  • Baseline — no interception. Browser fetches assets natively. Closest to real production traffic.
  • Baseline-CDP — same harness rig as TP (intercept + fulfill via CDP) but bytes refetched Node-side. Lets us isolate harness IPC tax. Network behaviour differs from baseline (no H/2 multiplexing on Node fetches).
  • TokenPack — intercept + serve from packed PNG. Pays harness IPC tax on every served asset.
  • Δ TP vs BL (prod fairness) — TP minus baseline. Real comparison including harness handicap. If TP wins here, it wins despite the rig.
  • Δ TP vs BL-CDP (tech fairness) — TP minus baseline-CDP. Both pay harness tax, so this isolates pack-vs-direct. Tech-level win.
  • Est. prod TP (–harness) — projection of TP duration with harness IPC tax stripped out. Computed as TP × (1 − harnessRatio) where harnessRatio = sum(harness)/sum(totalMs) across all served-from-pack assets. Approximates a real Service-Worker deployment where the browser delivers pack bytes directly without CDP roundtrips. Estimate, not measurement.
